Figure 12
.
The main preproof
P

Auxiliary formulas are grayed to emphasize the trajectory of the thread of interest.

The thread on the branch with infinitely many
(\star)
must use formula
F
, as it is the only one performing a
\nu
-unfolding. This means it has to go through the two cuts and bounce on axioms in
\pi_{M}
and
\pi_{R}
.
All other infinite branches are validated by non-bouncing threads.

As described in the outline, the goal of
\pi_{M}
is to use the bouncing thread stemming from
F
to simulate a run of
M
in a deterministic way, accumulating “garbage constraints” for every test. The role of
\pi_{R}
is to erase these garbage constraints, by mirroring the behaviour of
\pi_{M}
.

After
\pi_{R}
, the formula
F
is unfolded twice before looping back to the root. The first unfolding is used to match a leftover constraint, that cannot be erased in
\pi_{R}
for technical reasons detailed later. The second unfolding contributes to the visible part of the thread.

Encoding of counters in the constraint stack

We describe here how the preproof
\pi_{M}
will be able to simulate a run of
M
via a thread following formula
F
.

When
F
is unfolded, a thread following
F
can either go to the left disjunct or to the right, corresponding to weights
\mathtt{i}\mathtt{l}
or
\mathtt{i}\mathtt{r}
. We will use the alias
\mathbf{l}
for
\mathtt{i}\mathtt{l}
and
\mathbf{r}
for
\mathtt{i}\mathtt{r}
, since unfoldings will always alternate with left/right choices.
For instance if
u
is of the form
\mathbf{l}v
, then when the thread goes up and encounters an unfolding of
F
, it has to follow the left disjunct, and update the constraint from
u
to
v
. Constraints are updated according to the stack of the pushdown automaton
\mathcal{A}_{\mathit{thread}}
described in Section
6.1
. Therefore, we will refer to the word
u\in\{\mathbf{l},\mathbf{r}\}^{*}
storing the current constraints as the
constraint stack
.

We are now ready to detail how configurations of the machine will be encoded in the constraint stack.
A counter of value
n
will be encoded by the sequence of constraints
(\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}^{n}\mathbf{r})
. Therefore, when the thread is simulating the run of
M
, its constraint stack is of the form
(\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}^{n}\mathbf{r})(\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}^{m}\mathbf{r})
to denote that
k[1]=n
and
k[2]=m
.

The current state is not encoded in the constraint stack, but in the current position of the thread in the preproof: for instance if the thread is in the node labeled
(q_{0})
in the proof graph, then the current state of the corresponding run is
q_{0}
.

“Garbage constraints” are constraints that will not be part of the constraint stack during the simulation of the run of
M
, but will be pushed on the stack by the thread when the simulation has succeeded, i.e. after the node labeled
(q_{f})
has been reached by the thread. In order to exit the cut, the thread must go down from
(q_{f})
to
(q_{0})
, and that is where garbage constraints may be pushed. These extra constraints will encode the results of all tests performed during the computation. The result of a test is
\mathbf{r}
if the tested counter was Zero, and
\mathbf{l}
otherwise, so in general it is a letter
X\in\{\mathbf{r},\mathbf{l}\}
. Garbage constraints will be of the form
\mathbf{r}X
for a test on the first counter, and of the form
\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}^{k}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}X
for a test on second counter, where
X
is the result of the test and
k
is the value of the first counter.

Auxiliary metarules for
\pi_{M}

In order to make the construction readable and modular, we start by describing metarules that will be used as building blocks throughout the section.

Rules relative to
A

We will use the
\infty
notation to denote an infinite tree obtained by unfolding a proof of
A
. This yields a valid cut-free proof tree that contains no axiom.

We explicit this from sequent
\Gamma,A
, where
\Gamma
can be any sequent.

\begin{array}[]{rl}\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 13.54163pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=27.08327pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\infty)}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{}}\hbox{$\vdash\Gamma,A$}}}}}}$}\equiv&\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 137.15285pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=403.611pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\nu)}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 149.23616pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=552.84717pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{({\otimes},\bindnasrepma)}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 137.15285pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox{}\kern 15.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=404.30545pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\nu)}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 12.63887pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 12.0832pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=119.72223pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{({\otimes},\bindnasrepma)}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox{}\kern 15.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$(\vartriangle)$}}\hbox{$(\spadesuit)$}}\hbox{$(\spadesuit)$}}}}\hbox{$\vdash A,(A\bindnasrepma A){\otimes}A$}}}}\hbox{$(\spadesuit)\vdash A,A$}}}}}}\hbox{$\vdash\Gamma,(A\bindnasrepma A){\otimes}A$}}}}\hbox{$(\vartriangle)\vdash\Gamma,A$}}}}}}$}\par\end{array}

We will often want to duplicate
A
using its
\bindnasrepma
connective. Let us define the following metarule, valid for any sequent
\Gamma,A
:

\begin{array}[]{rl}\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=72.08318pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\bindnasrepma_{A})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 5.97221pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\vdash\Gamma,A,A$}}\hbox{$\vdash\Gamma,A$}}}}}}$}\equiv&\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 137.15285pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=393.8888pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\nu)}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 158.95837pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=552.84717pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{({\otimes})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 137.15285pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox{}\kern 15.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 8.19441pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=361.38884pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\infty)}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 4.30551pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=86.6664pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\bindnasrepma)}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\vdash\Gamma,A,A$}}\hbox{$\vdash\Gamma,A\bindnasrepma A$}}}}\hbox{}}\hbox{$\vdash A$}}}}}}\hbox{$\vdash\Gamma,(A\bindnasrepma A){\otimes}A$}}}}\hbox{$\vdash\Gamma,A$}}}}}}$}\par\end{array}

We will define an alias for a cut rule allowing to duplicate the
A
formula on both sides, noted
(\mathrm{Acut})
. The effect of rule
(\mathrm{Acut})
is simply a cut for formulas
F,G
, so it can be considered as such for threads of interests following
F
upwards and
G
downwards.

\begin{array}[]{rl}\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=133.63655pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mathrm{Acut})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 19.09094pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox{}\kern 15.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}$}\equiv&\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 13.95204pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=388.32077pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\bindnasrepma_{A})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 19.09094pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=138.77545pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(Cut)}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 13.95204pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox{}\kern 15.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}$}\par\end{array}

Copying left and right constraints

We now describe a helpful metarule: the expanding rule
{\scriptsize\mathsf{(\exp)}}
, represented in Fig.
13
. It allows to unfold
F
and
G
once, pairing the left (resp. right) unfolding of
F
with the left (resp. right) unfolding of
G
. This ensures that if the thread goes left (resp. right) upwards on
F
, it will also go left (resp. right) downwards on
G
. This can therefore be understood as a copying operator: the bit of the constraint stack will be the same before and after bouncing on the current cut.
As before, we will gray formulas that will always be avoided by the thread of interest.

~\hskip 113.81102pt\begin{array}[]{cc}\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=158.81978pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\exp)}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 27.48535pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox{}\kern 15.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\vdash G_{\mathbf{l}},F_{\mathbf{l}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}\hbox{$\vdash G_{\mathbf{r}},F_{\mathbf{r}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}$}\equiv&\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 22.34645pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=388.32077pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\bindnasrepma_{A})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 27.48535pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.55551pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=415.25061pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mu,\nu,\bindnasrepma)}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 22.34645pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=185.7496pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{({\otimes})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.55551pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox{}\kern 15.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\vdash G_{\mathbf{l}},F_{\mathbf{l}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}\hbox{$\vdash G_{\mathbf{r}},F_{\mathbf{r}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}\hbox{$\vdash G_{\mathbf{l}}{\otimes}G_{\mathbf{r}},F_{\mathbf{l}},F_{\mathbf{r}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}$}\end{array}

Figure 13
.
the metarule
{\scriptsize\mathsf{(\exp)}}

In most constructions, the left (resp. right) conjunct of
F
will be paired with the left (resp. right) conjunct of
G
when both are expanded, so we will often omit the labels. The left expansions will be represented on the left branch of the proof.

We give metarule
{\scriptsize\mathsf{(\mathbf{l})}}
(resp.
{\scriptsize\mathsf{(\mathbf{r})}}
) forcing the reading and copying of a left (resp. right) constraint on the stack. This is enforced by preventing the thread from bouncing on an axiom if the forbidden bit is read, thanks to the axiomless infinite proofs described by the
{\scriptsize\mathsf{(\infty)}}
metarules.

\begin{array}[]{rl}\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=76.36374pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mathbf{l})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}$}\equiv&\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=536.59094pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\exp)}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 153.40907pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox{}\kern 15.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 23.3326pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=92.04147pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\infty)}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\vdash G_{\mathbf{l}},F_{\mathbf{l}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}\hbox{}}\hbox{$\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}G_{\mathbf{r}},F_{\mathbf{r}},A}$}}}}}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}$}\par\\
\\
\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=76.36374pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mathbf{r})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}$}\equiv&\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=608.25537pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\exp)}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 177.29721pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox{}\kern 15.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 22.13258pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=44.26518pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\infty)}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{}}\hbox{$\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}G_{\mathbf{l}},F_{\mathbf{l}},A}$}}}}\hbox{$\vdash G_{\mathbf{r}},F_{\mathbf{r}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}$}\end{array}

Constraint introduction

We might also want to push a right constraint without popping one. For instance this is needed at the beginning when the stack is empty.
This can be done via the following metarules
\pi_{\mathbf{r}_{\mathsf{i}}}
and
(\mathbf{r}_{\mathsf{i}})
, where
\mathsf{i}
stands for ”Introduction”. We define both variants because we might want to use one or the other depending on the context.

Let
\pi_{\mathbf{r}_{\mathsf{i}}}
be the following preproof:

\begin{array}[]{cc}\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 13.27275pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=49.81824pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\pi_{\mathbf{r}_{\mathsf{i}}}$}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}$}\equiv&\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 141.94728pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=394.64366pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mu)}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 153.40907pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=548.05273pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{({\otimes})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 141.94728pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox{}\kern 15.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 16.62842pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=378.25684pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(Ax)}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 14.47983pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=28.95967pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\infty)}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{}}\hbox{$\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}G_{\mathbf{l}},A}$}}}}\hbox{}}\hbox{$\vdash G_{\mathbf{r}},F$}}}}}}\hbox{$\vdash G_{\mathbf{l}}{\otimes}G_{\mathbf{r}},F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}$}\end{array}

This preproof allows a thread to go upwards following
F
without any event, bounce on an axiom, and go downwards following
G
while pushing a right constraint.

We now combine
\pi_{\mathbf{r}_{\mathsf{i}}}
with a cut in order to go back to a thread going upwards, having a constraint stack starting with an extra
\mathbf{r}
.

\begin{array}[]{cc}\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=76.36374pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mathbf{r}_{\mathsf{i}})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}$}\equiv&\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=536.59094pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mathrm{Acut})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 153.40907pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox{}\kern 15.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 13.27275pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=88.0001pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}\hbox{$\pi_{\mathbf{r}_{\mathsf{i}}}$}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}$}\end{array}

This means that the effect of the rule
(\mathbf{r}_{\mathsf{i}})
on the constraint stack can be summarized by
\epsilon\mapsto\mathbf{r}
, adding an extra
\mathbf{r}
at the top of the constraint stack.

The above rule can be similarly defined to introduce a
\mathbf{l}
constraint instead, by simply switching the
G,F
axiom to the left premiss instead of the right in
\pi_{\mathbf{r}_{\mathsf{i}}}
. This dual version will be noted by
\pi_{\mathbf{l}_{\mathsf{i}}}
and metarule
(\mathbf{l}_{\mathsf{i}})
.

The initialization metarule

We describe here the first metarule encountered in
\pi_{M}
. Its role is to initialize the constraint stack to encode two counters with value
0
, and go to the
(q_{0})
node to start the simulation of the run of
M
.

It must therefore allow the thread to enter with empty constraint stack and exit the cut with a constraint stack
(\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r})(\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r})
.
This is straightforward now that we have the
(\mathbf{r}_{\mathsf{i}})
rule:

\begin{array}[]{cc}\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=106.16057pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mathsf{init})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 9.93228pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$(q_{0})\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}$}\equiv&\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 9.93228pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=383.18187pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mathbf{r}_{\mathsf{i}})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 9.93228pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 9.93228pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=383.18187pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mathbf{r}_{\mathsf{i}})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 9.93228pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 9.93228pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=383.18187pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mathbf{r}_{\mathsf{i}})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 9.93228pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=106.16057pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mathbf{r}_{\mathsf{i}})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 9.93228pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$(q_{0})\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}$}\end{array}

In the following, we describe how to build the preproof
\pi_{M}
, by connecting nodes of the form
(p)
to their successors in the computation.
So for each
p\in Q
, a preproof of “local root”
(p)
will be built with hypotheses of the form
(q)
with
q\in Q
. Once such a preproof has been built for each node
(p)
, the hypotheses
(q)
are connected to their corresponding “local root” node trough back loops.

Encoding the action
\mathrm{Inc}

We now assume that we are at a node of the proof graph labeled by
(p)
, where
p
is a state of the machine
M
, and that the current constraint stack encodes the counter value as described earlier, i.e.
(\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}^{k[1]}\mathbf{r})(\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}^{k[2]}\mathbf{r})
.

Assume
\delta(p)=\mathrm{Inc}_{1}(q)
with
q\in Q
.
We will build a metarule
(\mathrm{Inc}_{1})
updating the configuration by acting on the constraint stack, and ending up in node
(q)
:

\begin{array}[]{cc}\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.10417pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=107.32892pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mathsf{Inc}_{1})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$(q)\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}\hbox{$(p)\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}$}\equiv&\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=544.38432pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mathrm{Acut})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 145.61569pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox{}\kern 15.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=387.97908pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mathbf{r})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 4.79721pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 18.66997pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=111.77293pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$(q)\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}\hbox{$\pi_{\mathbf{l}_{\mathsf{i}}}$}}\hbox{$\vdash G_{\mathbf{r}},F_{\mathbf{r}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}\hbox{$(p)\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}$}\end{array}

Notice that in order to bounce on the axiom, the thread must see
\mathbf{r}
while going up, and
\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}
on the way down. The rest of the current stack (of the form
\mathbf{l}^{*}\mathbf{r}(\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}^{*}\mathbf{r})
) is left unchanged.
This rule turns a stack of the form
\mathbf{r}u
into
\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}u
, thereby incrementing the first counter. We will abbreviate this action
\mathbf{r}\mapsto\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}
.

We might also need to increment the second counter, which is deeper in the stack.
For this, let us devise another auxiliary metarule
({\mathsf{counter}})
, allowing us to skip the part of the stack encoding the first counter.

\begin{array}[]{cc}\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=76.36374pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{({\mathsf{counter}})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}$}\equiv&\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 30.17143pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=395.47906pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mathbf{r})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 42.46863pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=216.0668pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\exp)}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 30.17143pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox{}\kern 15.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$(\dagger)\vdash G_{\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}},F_{\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}\hbox{$\vdash G_{\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}},F_{\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}\hbox{$(\dagger)\vdash G_{\mathbf{r}},F_{\mathbf{r}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}$}\end{array}

This metarule processes constraints of the form
(\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}^{*}\mathbf{r})
on the way up, and these same constraints will be copied back when the thread returns downwards from the formula
G
of the hypothesis.
This means that this gadget allows the right premiss sequent to access the encoding of the second counter, while leaving the first one untouched.

We can now give the pre-proof allowing to increment the second counter, by simply adding the
({\mathsf{counter}})
metarule at the appropriate place:

\begin{array}[]{cc}\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.10417pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=107.32892pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mathsf{Inc}_{2})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$(q)\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}\hbox{$(p)\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}$}\equiv&\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=544.38432pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mathrm{Acut})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 145.61569pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox{}\kern 15.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 4.79721pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=383.18187pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{({\mathsf{counter}})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 4.79721pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=387.97908pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mathbf{r})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 4.79721pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 18.66997pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=111.77293pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$(q)\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}\hbox{$\pi_{\mathbf{l}_{\mathsf{i}}}$}}\hbox{$\vdash G_{\mathbf{r}},F_{\mathbf{r}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}\hbox{$(p)\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}$}\end{array}

The effect of this metarule is
(\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}^{*}\mathbf{r})\mathbf{r}\mapsto(\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}^{*}\mathbf{r})\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}

This achieves the treatment of states performing an increment. For all nodes
(p)
where
p
performs an increment of counter
\tau
before going to
q
, we link node
(p)
of the proof with node
(q)
through the metarule
(\mathrm{Inc}_{\tau})
.

Encoding the action
\mathrm{Dec}

Assume
\delta(p)=\mathrm{Dec}_{1}(q)
with
q\in Q
.

This means we want to build a metarule with action
\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}\mapsto\mathbf{r}
on the stack, in order to decrease the value of the first counter by
1
.

This is done by the following metarule
{\scriptsize\mathsf{(\mathsf{Dec}_{1})}}
:

\mathsf{(\mathrm{Acut})}

\mathsf{(\mu,\nu,\bindnasrepma)}

\mathsf{(\nu,\bindnasrepma)}

\mathsf{({\otimes})}

\mathsf{(Ax)}

\mathsf{(\infty)}

(q)\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}G_{\mathbf{l}},F_{\mathbf{l}},F_{\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}},A}

G_{\mathbf{r}},F_{\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}}

\vdash G_{\mathbf{l}}{\otimes}G_{\mathbf{r}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}F_{\mathbf{l}}},F_{\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}F_{\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}

\vdash G_{\mathbf{l}}{\otimes}G_{\mathbf{r}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}F_{\mathbf{l}}},F_{\mathbf{r}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}

\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}

(p)\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}

Notice that if the thread does not start with
\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}
, it gets lost in an
(\infty)
proof, corresponding to a failure of the run.

If
\delta(p)=\mathrm{Dec}_{2}(q)
, the construction is similar, using again the metarule
({\mathsf{counter}})
to leave the first counter untouched and access the second one. This is done by the following metarule
{\scriptsize\mathsf{(\mathsf{Dec}_{2})}}
.

\mathsf{(\mathrm{Acut})}

\mathsf{({\mathsf{counter}})}

\mathsf{(\mu,\nu,\bindnasrepma)}

\mathsf{(\nu,\bindnasrepma)}

\mathsf{({\otimes})}

\mathsf{(Ax)}

\mathsf{(\infty)}

(q)\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}G_{\mathbf{l}},F_{\mathbf{l}},F_{\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}},A}

G_{\mathbf{r}},F_{\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}}

\vdash G_{\mathbf{l}}{\otimes}G_{\mathbf{r}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}F_{\mathbf{l}}},F_{\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}F_{\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}

\vdash G_{\mathbf{l}}{\otimes}G_{\mathbf{r}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}F_{\mathbf{l}}},F_{\mathbf{r}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}

\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}

\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}

(p)\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}

Encoding the action
\mathrm{Test}

It remains to describe how to modify the constraint stack for actions of type
\mathrm{Test}
.

Test on the first counter

Let us assume first that
\delta(p)=\mathrm{Test}_{1}(q_{Z},q_{P})
.

Notice that a zero test can be performed by simply testing whether the stack starts with
\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}
or with
\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}
, i.e. by identifying the second letter of the stack.
Therefore, these first two letters can be used to branch to the result of the test.
However, since they must be still be part of the encoding, we need to reintroduce them in the stack after having read them.
We define the metarule
(\mathrm{Test}_{1})
accordingly:

\mathsf{(\mathbf{r})}

\mathsf{(\exp)}

\mathsf{(\mathbf{r}_{\mathsf{i}})}

\mathsf{(\mathbf{r}_{\mathsf{i}})}

\mathsf{(\mathbf{r}_{\mathsf{i}})}

\mathsf{(\mathbf{l}_{\mathsf{i}})}

(q_{P})\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}
\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}

\vdash G_{\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}},F_{\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}

(q_{Z})\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}
\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}

\vdash G_{\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}},F_{\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}

\vdash G_{\mathbf{r}},F_{\mathbf{r}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}

(p)\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}

This metarule allows the thread to go to
(q_{Z})
if the counter was zero, or to
(q_{P})
if the counter was strictly positive, leaving the stack unchanged in both cases.

Notice that this metarule also leaves some garbage constraints in the following sense: when going back down from
(q_{Z})
(resp.
(q_{P})
) to
(p)
, the thread will push extra constraints
\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}
(resp.
\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}
) on top of the pile, due to the
(\exp)
and
(\mathbf{r})
rule in
(\mathrm{Test}_{1})
.

Test on the second counter

We now assume that
\delta(p)=\mathrm{Test}_{2}(q_{Z},q_{P})
, and we want to encode the corresponding metarule, linking
(p)
to
(q_{Z})
and
(q_{P})
in the pre-proof
\pi_{M}
.

This is more tricky, because we need to access the relevant bit encoding the result of this test, and copy the value of the first counter after it to restore the stack. This corresponds to copying an unbounded amount of information, so this cannot be done directly in the same way as in the previous construction for
\mathrm{Test}_{1}
.

We therefore design auxiliary gadgets allowing us to copy the information bit by bit.
The result
T
of a test will be encoded by
T=\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}
for zero and
T=\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}
for not zero.

The pre-proof
\pi_{\mathrm{shift}}
, represented Fig.
14
has effect
\mathbf{l}^{k+1}T\mapsto\mathbf{l}^{k}T\mathbf{l}
, with
T\in\{\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r},\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}\}
.
The proof can be built thanks to the following table, that explicits the transformation of the relevant prefix constraint stack:

\begin{array}[]{|l|c|c|c|}\hline\cr\text{before: }&\mathbf{l}\mathbf{l}&\mathbf{l}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}&\mathbf{l}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}\\
\hline\cr\text{after: }&\mathbf{l}\mathbf{l}&\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}\mathbf{l}&\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{cc}\scalebox{1}{
\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 8.26436pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=59.835pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\pi_{\mathrm{shift}}$}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}$}
}\equiv&\scalebox{1}{
\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 153.40907pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=467.66415pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mathrm{Acut})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 222.33583pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox{}\kern 15.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 218.83096pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=388.32077pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\bindnasrepma_{A})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 223.96986pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 187.07893pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=425.2117pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mu,(\nu,\bindnasrepma)^{2})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 218.83096pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 141.1216pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=502.92105pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{({\otimes})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 187.07893pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox{}\kern 15.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 16.95467pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=411.72655pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=548.8784pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mu,{\otimes})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 141.1216pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox{}\kern 15.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 24.60342pt\hbox{}\hrule Figure 14
.
The pre-proof
\pi_{\mathrm{shift}}
with its auxiliary
\pi_{\mathsf{aux}}

Notice that all axioms in the right part of the cut in
\pi_{\mathrm{shift}}
are paired according to the table above.

The main interesting phenomenon in
\pi_{\mathrm{shift}}
occurs on the
(\bullet)
loop.
In the case where the result starts with
\mathbf{l}
, this loop allows to enter the cut again, after the popping of one
\mathbf{l}
constraint. This will therefore perform the wanted transformation on the constraint stack. Let us take an explicit example to see this gadget at work: consider a thread entering
\pi_{\mathrm{shift}}
with constraint
u=\mathbf{l}\mathbf{l}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}
.
The thread will bounce on axiom
G_{\mathbf{l}\mathbf{l}},F_{\mathbf{l}\mathbf{l}}
, leaving this constraint unchanged, and it will enter the
(\bullet)
node with constraint
\mathbf{l}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}
. This time, the detour will enter
\pi_{\mathsf{aux}}
, and will pop
\mathbf{l}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}
and push
\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}
onto the stack. When exiting the cut, the thread will have a constraint starting with
\mathbf{r}
and therefore will immediately bounce on the axiom. When going back, it will push back the first
\mathbf{l}
on the way down to the original root. It will finally exit with constraint
\mathbf{l}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}
, which is the wanted result of the mapping
\mathbf{l}^{k}T\mapsto\mathbf{l}^{k-1}T\mathbf{l}
.

We can now iterate
\pi_{\mathrm{shift}}
in order to move the result
T
on top of the stack.

Let us start with an auxiliary metarule
(\mathsf{copy}_{\mathbf{l}})
(Fig.
15
) copying the first letter of the stack if it is
\mathbf{l}
, and do nothing if the stack starts with
\mathbf{r}
. I.e. it has action
\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbf{l}\mapsto\mathbf{l}\mathbf{l}\\
\mathbf{r}\mapsto\mathbf{r}\end{array}\right.
on the stack. It will actually be the case that if the constraint starts with
\mathbf{r}
, it starts with
\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}
.

~\hskip 85.35826pt\begin{array}[]{cc}\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=76.36374pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mathsf{copy}_{\mathbf{l}})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}$}\equiv&\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=536.59094pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mathrm{Acut})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 153.40907pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox{}\kern 15.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 206.52406pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=404.97281pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mu,\nu,\bindnasrepma)}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 228.315pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 149.81187pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=483.47592pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{({\otimes})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 206.52406pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox{}\kern 15.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 18.74924pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=382.49849pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(Ax)}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=540.18814pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mu,{\otimes})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 149.81187pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox{}\kern 15.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 16.9062pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=378.81241pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\infty)}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 23.95895pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=86.09978pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(Ax)}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}\hbox{}}\hbox{$\vdash G_{\mathbf{l}\mathbf{l}},F_{\mathbf{l}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}\hbox{}}\hbox{$\vdash G_{\mathbf{l}\mathbf{r}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}\hbox{$\vdash G_{\mathbf{l}},F_{\mathbf{l}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}\hbox{}}\hbox{$\vdash G_{\mathbf{r}},F_{\mathbf{r}}$}}}}}}\hbox{$\vdash G_{\mathbf{l}}{\otimes}G_{\mathbf{r}},F_{\mathbf{l}},F_{\mathbf{r}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}$}\end{array}

Figure 15
.
The metarule
{\scriptsize\mathsf{(\mathsf{copy}_{\mathbf{l}})}}

We now build the proof
\pi_{\mathsf{move}}
(Fig.
16
), iterating
\pi_{\mathrm{shift}}
, allowing to copy an unbounded quantity of information past the test result
T
. The proof
\pi_{\mathsf{move}}
has the following action on the stack:
\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbf{l}^{k}T\mapsto\mathbf{l}^{k}T\mathbf{l}^{k}\\
\mathbf{r}\mapsto\mathbf{r}\end{array}\right.
. The principle is to first duplicate the leading
\mathbf{l}
, so that the extra occurrence can be used to test whether we want to perform a shifting using
\pi_{\mathrm{shift}}
.
If the stack starts with
\mathbf{r}
(actually with
\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}
), then the
{\scriptsize\mathsf{(\mathsf{copy}_{\mathbf{l}})}}
metarule will do nothing, and the thread will just bounce on the right axiom.
This allows us to iteratively call
\pi_{\mathrm{shift}}
, until we reach the encoded result
T
.
When exiting this gadget, the last
\pi_{\mathrm{shift}}
leaves constraint
T\mathbf{l}^{k}
, and one extra
\mathbf{l}
is collected by each
(\bigstar)
loop. That is why the constraint afer
\pi_{\mathsf{move}}
is
\mathbf{l}^{k}T\mathbf{l}^{k}
.

~\hskip 85.35826pt\begin{array}[]{cc}\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 8.29079pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=59.78217pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\pi_{\mathsf{move}}$}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}$}\equiv&\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 152.02376pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=403.18182pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mathsf{copy}_{\mathbf{l}})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 152.02376pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 17.22935pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=527.97626pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mu,\nu,\bindnasrepma,{\otimes})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 162.02374pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox{}\kern 15.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 18.74924pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=382.49849pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(Ax)}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=142.44058pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mathrm{Acut})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 17.22935pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox{}\kern 15.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$(\bigstar)\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}\hbox{$\pi_{\mathrm{shift}}$}}}}\hbox{$\vdash G_{\mathbf{l}},F_{\mathbf{l}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}\hbox{}}\hbox{$\vdash G_{\mathbf{r}},F_{\mathbf{r}}$}}}}}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}\hbox{$(\bigstar)\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}$}\end{array}

Figure 16
.
The metarule
\pi_{\mathsf{move}}

Another auxiliary metarule
(\mathsf{prep})
(Fig.
17
) will allow us to prepare the input for
\pi_{\mathsf{move}}
from the standard counter encoding, i.e. performing action
\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}^{k}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}X\mapsto\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}^{k}(\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}X)\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}X
, with
X\in\{\mathbf{r},\mathbf{l}\}
. The parenthesized expression is the
T
that we will want to move to the top. Notice that this corresponds to a copying of the counter
\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}^{k}\mathbf{r}
, followed by a mapping
\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}X\mapsto\mathbf{r}X\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}X
. The dots in Fig.
17
represents
(\infty)
proofs, not detailed for concision.

}\equiv&\scalebox{1}{

G_{\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}},F_{\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}}$}}}}\hbox{}}\hbox{$\vdash G_{\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}},F_{\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}}$}}}}\hbox{$\dots$}}}}}}\hbox{$\vdash G_{\mathbf{r}},F_{\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}},F_{\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}\hbox{$\vdash G,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}F_{\mathbf{l}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}F_{\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}}},F_{\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}},F_{\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}$}}\end{array}

Figure 17
.
The metarule
\mathsf{prep}

Let us now combine these gadgets to define a metarule
{\scriptsize\mathsf{(\mathsf{result})}}
, moving the test result at the wanted place, while producing extra stack content before it. This metarule has action
\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}^{k}T\mapsto\mathbf{l}^{k}T\mathbf{l}^{k}T
, with
T\in\{\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l},\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}\}
, and leaves a garbage constraint
\mathbf{r}
that will be seen later on the way down.

\begin{array}[]{cc}\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=76.36374pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mathsf{result})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}$}\equiv&\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 153.40907pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=383.18187pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mathsf{prep})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 153.40907pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 148.61186pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=387.97908pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mathbf{r})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 153.40907pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=541.38815pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mathrm{Acut})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 148.61186pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox{}\kern 15.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 8.29079pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=97.96404pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}\hbox{$\pi_{\mathsf{move}}$}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}\hbox{$\vdash G_{\mathbf{r}},F_{\mathbf{r}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}$}\end{array}

We can detail the stack modifications in
{\scriptsize\mathsf{(\mathsf{result})}}
:
(\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}^{k})T\xrightarrow[\,\,]{\,{\scriptsize\mathsf{(\mathsf{prep})}}\,}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}^{k}TT\xrightarrow[\,\,]{\,{\scriptsize\mathsf{(\mathbf{r})}}\,}\mathbf{l}^{k}TT\xrightarrow[\,\,]{\,\pi_{\mathsf{move}}\,}\mathbf{l}^{k}T\mathbf{l}^{k}T
.

We can finally build the metarule for
\mathrm{Test}_{2}
, performing the wanted test and leaving garbage constraint of the form
\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}^{k}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}X
with
X\in\{\mathbf{l},\mathbf{r}\}
.

\mathsf{(\mathsf{result})}

\mathsf{(\exp)}

\mathsf{(\mathbf{r})}

\mathsf{(\exp)}

\mathsf{(\mathbf{r}_{\mathsf{i}})}

\mathsf{(\mathbf{r}_{\mathsf{i}})}

(\clubsuit)\vdash G_{\mathbf{l}},F_{\mathbf{l}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}
(q_{P})\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}
\vdash G_{\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}},F_{\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}

(q_{Z})\vdash G_{,}F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}
\vdash G_{\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}},F_{\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}

\vdash G_{\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}},F_{\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}

\vdash G_{\mathbf{r}},F_{\mathbf{r}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}

(\clubsuit)\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}

(p)\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}

The principle of this gadget is the following: after preparing the stack via the
{\scriptsize\mathsf{(\mathsf{result})}}
metarule, the
(\clubsuit)
loops and the
{\scriptsize\mathsf{(\mathbf{r})}}
rule first pop the garbage prefix
\mathbf{l}^{k}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}
. The following bit
X\in\{\mathbf{l},\mathbf{r}\}
is the wanted test result, and allows us to enter
(q_{Z})
or
(q_{P})
with a remaining stack that encodes the next configuration of the machine (after adding the leading
\mathbf{r}
to complete the valid encoding).

Final state
q_{f}

It remains to describe what happens to a thread entering the node labelled by the final state
q_{f}
. We will simply allow it to finally bounce on an axiom, thereby starting a downwards path that will gather all the garbage constraints, exit
\pi_{M}
, and enter the second cut and the proof
\pi_{R}
.

We just need to evacuate the formula
A
from the sequent. Since we know that the constraint stack starts with
\mathbf{r}
, this can be done in the following way:

\mathsf{(\mu,\nu,\bindnasrepma,{\otimes})}

\mathsf{(Ax)}

\mathsf{(\infty)}

\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}G_{\mathbf{l}},F_{\mathbf{l}},A}

\vdash G_{\mathbf{r}},F_{\mathbf{r}}

(q_{f})\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}

Exiting the proof
\pi_{M}

This concludes the description of the proof
\pi_{M}
, starting with

\mathsf{(\mathsf{init})}

(q_{0})\vdash G,F,A
\vdash G,F,A

, and built as described by linking state-labelled nodes using rules we defined for the action performed by each state according to
\delta
.

This construction ensures the following Lemma:

Lemma A.36.

A thread entering
\pi_{M}
with empty constraint will be able to exit it if and only if
M
has a run.
If this is the case, the constraint after the exit is a word
u_{1}u_{2}\dots u_{k}\mathbf{r}^{4}
, where
k
is the number of tests performed, and
u_{i}
encodes the results
X\in\{\mathbf{l},\mathbf{r}\}
of the
i^{\mathit{th}}
test in the following way:

•

if the test is on the first counter, then
u_{i}=\mathbf{r}X
,

•

if the test is on the second counter, then
u_{i}=\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}^{k}\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}X
, where
k
is the value of the first counter at the time of the test.

Proof.

It is straightforward to prove by induction of the length of the thread/run: a thread entering
\pi_{M}
with empty constraint can reach node
(p)
with constraint
(\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}^{n}\mathbf{r})(\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}^{m}\mathbf{r})
if and only if their is a partial run of
M
reaching state
p
with counter values
n,m
. Moreover, the garbage constraints that will be pushed back from
(p)
on the way back to the root of
\pi_{M}
encode the results of tests as described in the statement of the Lemma.
The
\mathbf{r}^{4}
following garbage constraints is the constraint stack reached in
(q_{f})
at the end of computation, encoding two counters of value
0
, as we assumed
M
ends with this configuration.
∎

However, we want the thread to be back on the main branch with a bounded number of constraints. We therefore must erase all these garbage constraints. This will be the role of the pre-proof
\pi_{R}
.

The reverse simulation proof
\pi_{R}

The goal of the proof
\pi_{R}
will be to erase the garbage constraints instead of creating them.
To achieve this, we will aim at building a dual version of the pre-proof
\pi_{M}
.
A convenient way to think about it is the following: we try to reproduce the proof
\pi_{M}
, but considering this time that the thread of interest
t^{\prime}
originates in the sequent
G
. Therefore it will always follow
G
upwards and
F
downwards. We call such a thread a
G
-thread, and the previous version used in
\pi_{M}
a
F
-thread.

The principle is that if the
G
-thread
t^{\prime}
creates garbage constraints
u
, i.e. has a visible weight
u\in\Sigma^{*}
then its dual, the identical thread considered in reverse and originating in
F
, has a visible weight
\mathit{dual}(u)
, where the function
\mathit{dual}:\Sigma^{*}\to\Sigma^{*}
is defined as follows.
Let
v\mapsto\bar{v}:\Sigma^{*}\to\Sigma^{*}
be the length-preserving morphism defined on letters by the following correspondence:

\begin{array}[]{|l||c|c|c|c|c|c|c|c|c|}\hline\cr&&&&&&&&&\\[-10.00002pt]
x:&~l&~r&~i&~\bar{l}&~\bar{r}&~\bar{i}&~A&~C&~W\\
\hline\cr&&&&&&&&&\\[-10.00002pt]
\bar{x}:&~\bar{l}&~\bar{r}&~\bar{i}&~l&~r&~i&~A&~C&~W\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

Let
v^{R}
be the reverse of a word
v
, defined by induction:
\epsilon^{R}=\epsilon
and if
(u,a)\in\Sigma^{*}\times\Sigma
then
(ua)^{R}=a(u^{R})
.

We now define
\mathit{dual}(u)=\bar{u}^{R}
.

Lemma A.37.

Let
t
be a thread from
\varphi_{\alpha}
to
\psi_{\beta}
, and
\mathit{dual}(t)
be the identical thread considered in the other direction, from
\psi_{\beta}
to
\varphi_{\alpha}
.
Then
\mathsf{vp}(t^{R})=\mathit{dual}(\mathsf{vp}(t))

Let
t_{M}
be the
F
-thread of the main pre-proof
P
going through the
\pi_{M}
cut and ending with the cut rule, and
t_{R}
be the analog
F
-thread for the
\pi_{R}
cut. We want to build
\pi_{R}
such that
\mathsf{vp}((t_{R})^{R})=\mathsf{vp}(t_{M})
. By Lemma
A.37
, this implies
\mathsf{vp}(t_{R})=\mathit{dual}(\mathsf{vp}(t_{M}))
, and moreover
\mathsf{vp}(t_{M})\subseteq\{\bar{l},\bar{r},\bar{i}\}^{*}
, and
\mathsf{w}(t_{M})
ends with
C
. Therefore
\mathsf{vp}(t_{M}t_{R})=\epsilon
.

In the following, we will describe how gadgets of
\pi_{M}
are dualized to create
\pi_{R}
, where the run of the machine is simulated by a
G
-thread.

Dual auxiliary metarules

Rules relatives to
A
are left unchanged, so we will freely used
{\scriptsize\mathsf{(\infty)}},{\scriptsize\mathsf{(\bindnasrepma_{A})}},{\scriptsize\mathsf{(\mathrm{Acut})}}
.

The rules
{\scriptsize\mathsf{(\exp)}},{\scriptsize\mathsf{(\mathbf{r})}},{\scriptsize\mathsf{(\mathbf{l})}}
also stay identical.

The main difference will occur when we want to introduce or delete a constraint, since they will now be reversed.
In particular, in the previous construction, we saw that introducing a constraint could be done at will without any assumption, but removing a constraint as done in
{\scriptsize\mathsf{(\mathsf{Dec}_{1})}}
needed the presence of a known bit occuring before the constraint to be removed.

We therefore redefine constraint introduction gadgets
\pi_{\mathbf{r}_{\mathsf{i}}^{\prime}}
and
{\scriptsize\mathsf{(\mathbf{r}_{\mathsf{i}}^{\prime})}}
, with the notable change that the effect on stack is now
\mathbf{r}\mapsto\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}
, i.e. we always assume that the stack starts with
\mathbf{r}
.
We keep the convention for cuts, i.e. the newly introduced cut formulas are written on the inside (left
F
and right
G
).

\begin{array}[]{cc}\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 12.76753pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=50.82867pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\pi_{\mathbf{r}_{\mathsf{i}}^{\prime}}$}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}$}\equiv&\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 132.45145pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=404.1395pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mu,\nu,\bindnasrepma)}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 153.40907pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 121.89035pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=435.6582pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\nu,\bindnasrepma)}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 132.45145pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=568.10965pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{({\otimes})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 121.89035pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox{}\kern 15.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 16.17566pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=377.35132pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(Ax)}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 28.60062pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{}}\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}G_{\mathbf{l}},F_{\mathbf{l}},F_{\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}},A}$}}}}\hbox{}}\hbox{$G_{\mathbf{r}},F_{\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}}$}}}}}}\hbox{$\vdash G_{\mathbf{l}}{\otimes}G_{\mathbf{r}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}F_{\mathbf{l}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}F_{\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}}},F_{\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}\hbox{$\vdash G_{\mathbf{l}}{\otimes}G_{\mathbf{r}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}F_{\mathbf{l}}},F_{\mathbf{r}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}$}\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{cc}\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=76.36374pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mathbf{r}_{\mathsf{i}}^{\prime})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}$}\equiv&\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=593.86374pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mathrm{Acut})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 172.5pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{\hbox{}\kern 15.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 12.76753pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=50.82867pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\pi_{\mathbf{r}_{\mathsf{i}}^{\prime}}$}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}$}\end{array}

It is easily verified that a
G
-thread entering with constraint
\mathbf{r}
will exit with constraint
\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}
.

As before, we also define the left analog
\pi_{\mathbf{l}_{\mathsf{i}}^{\prime}}
and
{\scriptsize\mathsf{(\mathbf{l}_{\mathsf{i}}^{\prime})}}
, with effect
\mathbf{r}\mapsto\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}
.

Initialisation of
\pi_{R}

We now want to initialize the dual thread. However, since the introduction rule needs to assume an
\mathbf{r}
constraint already on the stack, we will need to assume this for the
G
-thread entering
\pi_{R}
. This means that in the end, the
F
-thread exiting
\pi_{R}
via
G
(the real thread of interest) will have a leftover constraint
\mathbf{r}
, that we will need to evacuate on the main branch, as done in the main pre-proof
P
.

Thus, we only need to add three
\mathbf{r}
constraints to the one already assumed:

\begin{array}[]{cc}\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=106.16057pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mathsf{init^{\prime}})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 9.93228pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$(q_{0}^{\prime})\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}$}\equiv&\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 9.93228pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=383.18187pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mathbf{r}_{\mathsf{i}}^{\prime})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 9.93228pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 9.93228pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=383.18187pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mathbf{r}_{\mathsf{i}}^{\prime})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 9.93228pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=106.16057pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mathbf{r}_{\mathsf{i}}^{\prime})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 9.93228pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$(q_{0}^{\prime})\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}\hbox{$\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}$}\end{array}

We will use the
(p^{\prime})
notation with
p\in Q
for state-labelled nodes of
\pi_{R}
, to distinguish them from nodes in
\pi_{M}
.

Dual encoding of action
\mathrm{Inc}

Assume
\delta(p)=\mathrm{Inc}_{1}(q)
with
q\in Q
.
We want to define a metarule
(\mathrm{Inc}_{1}^{\prime})
updating the configuration reached in
(p^{\prime})
by acting on the constraint stack, and ending up in node
(q^{\prime})
, with effect
\mathbf{r}\mapsto\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}
on the stack. Therefore it suffices to use the
\mathbf{l}
introduction defined before:

\begin{array}[]{cc}\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.10417pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=112.94003pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mathsf{Inc^{\prime}}_{1})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$(q^{\prime})\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}\hbox{$(p^{\prime})\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}$}\equiv&\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\vbox{\moveright 0.10417pt\hbox{}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hrule width=112.94003pt,height=0.0pt\hrule height=0.4pt\kern 3.0pt}}$}\kern 5.0pt\hbox{{\scriptsize$\mathsf{(\mathbf{l}_{\mathsf{i}}^{\prime})}$}}}}\hrule height=0.0pt\moveright 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$(q^{\prime})\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}\hbox{$(p^{\prime})\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}$}}}}}}$}\end{array}

To manipulate the second counter instead, we can use the
{\scriptsize\mathsf{({\mathsf{counter}})}}
rule which is identical as in
\pi_{M}
. Thus the metarule
{\scriptsize\mathsf{(\mathsf{Inc^{\prime}}_{2})}}
is defined as follows:

\mathsf{(\mathrm{Acut})}

\mathsf{({\mathsf{counter}})}

\pi_{\mathbf{l}_{\mathsf{i}}^{\prime}}
\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}

\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}

(q^{\prime})\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}
(p^{\prime})\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}

The effect of this metarule on a
G
-thread is
(\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}^{*}\mathbf{r})\mathbf{r}\mapsto(\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}^{*}\mathbf{r})\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}

Dual encoding of action
\mathrm{Dec}

We now need to encode a metarule having the effect
\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}\mapsto\mathbf{r}
on
G
-thread.

This is done by metarule
{\scriptsize\mathsf{(\mathsf{Dec^{\prime}}_{1})}}

If the
G
-thread enters with constraint prefix
\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}
, this prefix will be copied, the first copy will be read, put in the garbage to be collected later, and the
G
-thread will enter
(q_{Z}^{\prime})
. Similarly if the constraint started with
\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}
, the
G
-thread will go to
(q_{P}^{\prime})
with unchanged stack and garbage
\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}
.

To treat the case of the second counter, i.e.
\delta(p)=\mathrm{Test}_{2}(q_{Z},q_{P})

(\mathrm{Test}_{2})
in
\pi_{M}
. We design them so that the effect on the
G
-thread is exactly the same as the one of their original version on the
F
-thread.

The only difficulty to keep in mind is that the introduction rules now assume that the constraint stack starts with
\mathbf{r}
.

(\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}^{k}\mathbf{r})\mathbf{r}X\mapsto(\mathbf{r}\mathbf{l}^{k}\mathbf{r})\mathbf{r}X\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}X
, with
X\in\{\mathbf{l},\mathbf{r}\}
.
We will note
\Gamma_{F}
for a sequent composed of several non-pertinent occurrences of formula

, for concision.

We can finally build the metarule for
\mathrm{Test}_{2}
(Fig.

), performing the wanted test and leaving garbage constraint of the form

.

.
The metarule
{\scriptsize\mathsf{(\mathrm{Test}^{\prime}_{2})}}

After entering
{\scriptsize\mathsf{(\mathrm{Test}^{\prime}_{2})}}
with stack content
u
, the
G
-thread will reach node
(q_{Z}^{\prime})
(resp.
(q_{P}^{\prime})
) if the second counter value is zero (resp. not zero), with same stack content
u
.

Dual final state
q_{f}^{\prime}

As before, when reaching the node
(q_{f}^{\prime})
, we just need bounce on an axiom after evacuating the formula
A
from the sequent. The same gadget as in
\pi_{M}
can be used:

\mathsf{(\mu,\nu,\bindnasrepma,{\otimes})}

\mathsf{(Ax)}

\mathsf{(\infty)}

\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}G_{\mathbf{l}},F_{\mathbf{l}},A}

\vdash G_{\mathbf{r}},F_{\mathbf{r}}

(q_{f}^{\prime})\vdash G,F,{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}A}

Correctness of the pre-proof
P
and conclusion

By construction, if the machine
M
does not halt, then the
F
-thread entering
\pi_{M}
will never exit it, so it cannot validate the branch looping through the
(\star)
nodes, and the pre-proof
P
is not a proof.
Conversely, if
M
halts, then the
F
-thread will exit
\pi_{M}
with a constraint
u
encoding the results of the tests as described in Lemma
A.36
. Moreover, the
G
-thread
t^{\prime}
entering
\pi_{R}
with a single constraint
\mathbf{r}
will exit on the same node with the same constraints
u
. This means that the
F
-thread going through
\pi_{M}
and
\pi_{R}
will exit
\pi_{R}
with a single constraint
\mathbf{r}
. This constraint will be popped on the first unfolding of
F
, and the second (left) unfolding of
F
will be on the visible part of the thread. The thread then reaches node
(\star)
, and loops back to the root to go through the same path infinitely many times.

Thus the
F
-thread starting in the root validates the branch containing infinitely many
(\star)
nodes. All other infinite branches of
P
are validated by non-bouncing thread following formulas
A
, that can also be originated in the root of
P
, in the formula
B
.

Therefore, we showed the following theorem:

Theorem A.38.

The pre-proof
P
is a proof if and only if the 2CM
M
has a run.

By Theorem
A.35
, we obtain that deciding whether a circular pre-proof of
\mu\mathsf{MLL}^{\omega}
is a proof is undecidable, and more precisely it is
\Sigma^{0}_{1}
-hard.
In the next section, we show that this problem is recursively enumerable, so we obtain Cor.
6.6
, stating that deciding validity of a circular pre-proof of
\mu\mathsf{MLL}^{\omega}
is
\Sigma^{0}_{1}
-complete.
